<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which molecular function best describes this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Dynein light chain binding<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Dynein intermediate chain binding<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Tubulin binding<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Axonemal microtubule binding<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: Analyzing the protein sequence reveals several key features:<br><br>- **Large size**: The protein is approximately 800+ amino acids, which is consistent with dynein intermediate chain (DIC) proteins.<br>- **Coiled-coil rich structure**: The sequence is dominated by hydrophobic amino acids (Leu, Ala, Val) arranged in heptad repeat patterns characteristic of extensive coiled-coil domains. This is a hallmark of dynein intermediate chains.<br>- **Leucine-rich regions**: Multiple stretches with high leucine content in patterns suggestive of coiled-coil and leucine zipper-like motifs.<br>- **High alpha-helical propensity**: The amino acid composition strongly suggests a predominantly alpha-helical protein with extended coiled-coil regions.<br><br>The overall architecture — a large, elongated coiled-coil protein — is most consistent with a **dynein intermediate chain (DIC)** protein. Dynein intermediate chains serve as scaffold proteins in the dynein motor complex and are known to bind multiple copies of dynein light chains (such as TCTEX1, roadblock, LC8). Therefore, the key molecular function of this protein is **dynein light chain binding**.<br><br>Option B (dynein intermediate chain binding) would describe a protein that binds *to* the intermediate chain, not the intermediate chain itself.
